{"forms":[{"form":"Galois fields","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"The Galois field #92;mathrm#123;GF#125;(pⁿ) has order pⁿ and characteristic p.","type":"example"},{"text":"The Galois field #92;mathrm#123;GF#125;(pⁿ) is a finite extension of the Galois field #92;mathrm#123;GF#125;(p) and the degree of the extension is n.","type":"example"},{"text":"The multiplicative subgroup of a Galois field is cyclic.","type":"example"},{"text":"A Galois field #92;mathbb#123;F#125;#95;#123;pⁿ#125; is isomorphic to the quotient of the polynomial ring #92;mathbb#123;F#125;#95;p adjoin x over the ideal generated by a monic irreducible polynomial of degree n. Such an ideal is maximal and since a polynomial ring is commutative then the quotient ring must be a field. In symbols: #92;mathbb#123;F#125;#95;#123;pⁿ#125;#92;cong#123;#92;mathbb#123;F#125;#95;p#91;x#93;#92;over(#92;hatf#95;n(x))#125;.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A finite field; a field that contains a finite number of elements."],"topics":["algebra","mathematics","sciences"]}],"upstream_line":6076554,"upstream_offset":13626271767,"upstream_record_sha256":"9662870b8374225021f4e2f2e978b0e1d702efcd05449967a46349a20c94d490","word":"Galois field"}

{"etymology_number":"1","forms":[{"form":"further gone","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"farther gone","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"goner","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"furthest gone","tags":["superlative"]},{"form":"farthest gone","tags":["superlative"]},{"form":"gonest","tags":["superlative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"adj","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"Are they gone already?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Away, having left."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The days of my youth are gone.","type":"example"},{"text":"All hopes for a truce are gone.","type":"example"},{"text":"All the little shops that used to be here are now gone.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["No longer existing, having passed."]},{"examples":[{"text":"I'm afraid all the coffee is gone.","type":"example"},{"text":"If the milk is all gone, we'll drink our coffee black.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Used up."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The bulb is gone. It's through the floor. They're gone.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Doomed, done for."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Don't bother trying to understand what Grandma says; she's gone.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Not fully aware of one's surroundings, often through intoxication or mental decline."],"tags":["colloquial"]},{"glosses":["Infatuated; in love (+ on, for, in)."],"tags":["slang"]},{"examples":[{"text":"It was a group of real gone cats.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Excellent, wonderful; crazy."],"tags":["US","dated","informal"]},{"glosses":["Ago (used post-positionally)."],"tags":["archaic"]},{"glosses":["Weak; faint; feeling a sense of goneness."],"tags":["US"]},{"glosses":["Of an arrow: wide of the mark."]},{"examples":[{"text":"She’s three months gone","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Used with a duration to indicate for how long a process has been developing, an action has been performed or a state has persisted; especially, pregnant."]}],"sounds":[{"enpr":"gŏn","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/ɡɒn/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/ɡɔːn/","tags":["Australia","Multicultural-London-English","Received-Pronunciation","archaic"]},{"enpr":"gôn","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"/ɡɔn/","tags":["General-American"]},{"enpr":"gŏn","tags":["New-York-City","cot-caught-merger","traditional"]},{"ipa":"/ɡɑn/","tags":["New-York-City","cot-caught-merger","traditional"]}],"upstream_line":30973,"upstream_offset":209507772,"upstream_record_sha256":"d407454dccb3316ea37ee5570c158e3b03c15e8082b231c5f02c9c5073e4b06c","word":"gone"}

individual."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The invention was born of necessity.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing origin.","Indicating a (non-physical) source of action or emotion; introducing a cause, instigation; from, out of, as an expression of."]},{"examples":[{"text":"It is said that she died of a broken heart.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing origin.","Indicates the source or cause of the verb."],"qualifier":"following an intransitive verb"},{"examples":[{"text":"I am tired of all this nonsense.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing origin.","Indicates the subject or cause of the adjective."],"qualifier":"following an adjective"},{"examples":[{"text":"I am not particularly enamoured of this idea.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing agency.","Indicates the agent (for most verbs, now usually expressed with by)."],"qualifier":"following a passive verb"},{"examples":[{"text":"The contract can be terminated at any time with the agreement of both parties.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing agency.","Used to introduce the \"subjective genitive\"; following a noun to form the head of a postmodifying noun phrase (see also 'Possession' senses below)."]},{"examples":[{"text":"It was very brave of you to speak out like that.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing agency.","Used to indicate the agent of something described by the adjective."],"qualifier":"following an adjective"},{"examples":[{"text":"Many 'corks' are now actually made of plastic.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing composition, substance.","Used to indicate the material or substance used."],"qualifier":"making etc.","raw_tags":["after a verb expressing construction"]},{"examples":[{"text":"She wore a dress of silk.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing composition, substance.","Used to indicate the material of the just-mentioned object."],"qualifier":"directly following a noun"},{"examples":[{"text":"I'll have a dozen of those apples, please.","type":"example"},{"text":"What a lot of nonsense!","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing composition, substance.","Indicating the composition of a given collective or quantitative noun."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Germany's formal name is the Federal Republic of Germany.","type":"example"},{"text":"Welcome to the historic town of Harwich.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing composition, substance.","Used to link a given class of things with a specific example of that class."]},{"examples":[{"text":"I'm not driving this wreck of a car.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing composition, substance.","Links two nouns in near-apposition, with the first qualifying the second; \"which is also\"."]},{"examples":[{"text":"I'm always thinking of you.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Introducing subject matter.","Links an intransitive verb, or a transitive verb and its subject (especially verbs to do with thinking, feeling, expressing etc.), with its subject-matter; concerning, with regard to."]},{"examples":[{"text":"He told us the story of his journey to India.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Introducing subject matter.","Introduces its subject matter; about, concerning."],"qualifier":"following a noun (now chiefly nouns of knowledge, communication etc.)"},{"examples":[{"text":"This behaviour is typical of teenagers.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Introducing subject matter.","Introduces its subject matter."],"qualifier":"following an adjective"},{"examples":[{"text":"Most of these apples are rotten.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Having partitive effect.","Introduces the whole for which is indicated only the specified part or segment; \"from among\"."],"qualifier":"following a number or other quantitive word"},{"examples":[{"text":"We want a larger slice of the cake.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Having partitive effect.","Indicates a given part."],"qualifier":"following a noun"},{"examples":[{"text":"On the whole, they seem to be of the decent sort.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Having partitive effect.","Some, an amount of, one of."],"qualifier":"or as a predicate after to be","raw_tags":["with preceding partitive word assumed"],"tags":["archaic","literary"]},{"examples":[{"text":"He is a friend of mine.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Having partitive effect.","Links to a genitive noun or possessive pronoun, with partitive effect (though now often merged with possessive senses, below; see also double possessive)."]},{"examples":[{"text":"He was perhaps the most famous scientist of the twentieth century.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing possession.","Belonging to, existing in, or taking place in a given location, place or time. Compare \"origin\" senses, above."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The owner of the nightclub was arrested.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing possession.","Belonging to (a place) through having title, ownership or control over it."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Keep the handle of the saucepan away from the flames.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing possession.","Belonging to (someone or something) as something they possess or have as a characteristic; the \"possessive genitive\". (With abstract nouns, this intersects with the subjective genitive, above under \"agency\" senses.)"]},{"glosses":["Forming the \"objective genitive\"."]},{"examples":[{"text":"She had a profound distrust of the police.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Forming the \"objective genitive\".","Follows an agent noun, verbal noun or noun of action."]},{"examples":[{"text":"My companion seemed affable and easy of manner.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing qualities or characteristics.","Links an adjective with a noun or noun phrase to form a quasi-adverbial qualifier; in respect to, as regards."],"tags":["archaic","literary"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Pooh was said to be a bear of very little brain.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing qualities or characteristics.","Indicates a quality or characteristic; \"characterized by\"."]},{"examples":[{"text":"A young man of 20 has been paying interest at an exorbitant rate of 10%.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing qualities or characteristics.","Indicates quantity, age, price, etc."]},{"examples":[{"text":"It's not that big of a deal. (Standard form: It's not that big a deal.)","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing qualities or characteristics.","Used to link singular indefinite nouns (preceded by the indefinite article) and attributive adjectives modified by certain common adverbs of degree."],"qualifier":"considered incorrect by some","tags":["US","informal"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Of an evening, we would often go for a stroll along the river.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing a point in time.","During the course of (a set period of time, day of the week etc.), now specifically with implied repetition or regularity."],"tags":["regional"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I’ve not taken her out of a goodly long while.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing a point in time.","For (a given length of time)."],"raw_tags":["in the negative"],"tags":["UK","dialectal"]},{"examples":[{"text":"After a delay of three hours, the plane finally took off.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing a point in time.","Indicates duration of a state, activity etc."],"raw_tags":["after a noun"]},{"examples":[{"text":"What's the time? / Nearly a quarter of three.","type":"example"},{"text":"I'll be ready by five of noon.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Expressing a point in time.","Before (the hour); to (the hour)."],"tags":["US"]},{"glosses":["Expressing a point in time.","Before (the hour); to (the hour).","Often used without the hour."],"tags":["US","informal"]}],"sounds":[{"enpr":"ŏv"},{"ipa":"/ɒv/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/ɔv/","tags":["Australia","British","New-Zealand","Southern","Standard"]},{"enpr":"ŭv"},{"ipa":"/ʌv/","tags":["US"]},{"ipa":"[ɐv~ʌv~əv]","tags":["US"]},{"enpr":"ŏf"},{"ipa":"/ɔf/","tags":["South-Asia"]},{"enpr":"əv"},{"ipa":"/əv/","tags":["Western"]},{"ipa":"/ə/","tags":["Western"]},{"enpr":"əf"},{"ipa":"/əf/","tags":["South-Asia"]},{"ipa":"/ə/","tags":["South-Asia"]}],"upstream_line":14507,"upstream_offset":98414446,"upstream_record_sha256":"6dc07e78fed50b35966349fddb0e2fe89040668341dab8923ad7c86130ac6bda","word":"of"}
